=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
qwen

=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import com.google.gson.Gson;

import java.io.*;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Paths;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;

public class JSONProcessor {

    public Map<String, Object> readJson(String filePath) {
        if (!Files.exists(Paths.get(filePath))) {
            return null;
        }
        try (BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(filePath))) {
            return new Gson().fromJson(reader, Map.class);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return null;
        }
    }

    public boolean writeJson(Map<String, Object> data, String filePath) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(filePath))) {
            new Gson().toJson(data, writer);
            return true;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public boolean processJson(String filePath, String removeKey) {
        Map<String, Object> data = readJson(filePath);
        if (data == null) {
            return false;
        }
        if (data.containsKey(removeKey)) {
            data.remove(removeKey);
            return writeJson(data, filePath);
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }
}

=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to cpp.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic cpp only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import com.google.gson.Gson;

import java.io.*;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Paths;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;

public class JSONProcessor {

    public Map<String, Object> readJson(String filePath) {
        if (!Files.exists(Paths.get(filePath))) {
            return null;
        }
        try (BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(filePath))) {
            return new Gson().fromJson(reader, Map.class);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return null;
        }
    }

    public boolean writeJson(Map<String, Object> data, String filePath) {
        try (BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(filePath))) {
            new Gson().toJson(data, writer);
            return true;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public boolean processJson(String filePath, String removeKey) {
        Map<String, Object> data = readJson(filePath);
        if (data == null) {
            return false;
        }
        if (data.containsKey(removeKey)) {
            data.remove(removeKey);
            return writeJson(data, filePath);
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }
}
```

Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior.

Keep the reasoning compact and single-pass:
- Identify only the pieces that must remain equivalent: signatures, I/O contract, stateful behavior, control flow, and key API semantics.
- Turn that mapping into target-language structure promptly.
- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.
- If a mismatch appears, patch only the affected local section and recheck once.
- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

Output only the translated cpp code in a code block.

```
bool writeJson(const std::map<std::string, json>& data, const std::string& filePath) {
    try {
        std::ofstream writer(filePath);
        if (!writer.is_open()) return false;
        writer.exceptions(std::ofstream::failbit | std::ofstream::badbit);
        json j(data);
        writer << j.dump();
        return true;
    } catch (...) {
        return false;
    }
}
```

```
std::optional<std::map<std::string, json>> readJson(const std::string& filePath) {
    if (!std::filesystem::exists(filePath)) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    try {
        std::ifstream reader(filePath);
        if (!reader.is_open()) return std::nullopt;
        json parsed = json::parse(reader);
        if (!parsed.is_object()) return std::nullopt;
        return parsed.get<std::map<std::string, json>>();
    } catch (...) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
}
```

We can use `writer << data.dump(); return static_cast<bool>(writer);` But if we set exceptions, `operator<<` throws on failure. Let's implement with exceptions:

```
bool writeJson(const ordered_json& data, const std::string& filePath) {
    try {
        std::ofstream writer(filePath);
        if (!writer.is_open()) return false;
        writer.exceptions(std::ofstream::failbit | std::ofstream::badbit);
        writer << data.dump();
        return true;
    } catch (...) {
        return false;
    }
}
```

```
std::optional<ordered_json> readJson(const std::string& filePath) {
    try {
        if (!std::filesystem::exists(filePath)) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
        std::ifstream reader(filePath);
        if (!reader.is_open()) return std::nullopt;
        ordered_json parsed = ordered_json::parse(reader);
        if (!parsed.is_object()) return std::nullopt;
        return parsed;
    } catch (...) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
}
```

Need maybe include `using Json = nlohmann::ordered_json;` to shorten. Then class:

```
#include <nlohmann/json.hpp>
#include <filesystem>
#include <fstream>
#include <optional>
#include <string>

class JSONProcessor {
public:
    std::optional<nlohmann::ordered_json> readJson(const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            if (!std::filesystem::exists(filePath)) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }
            std::ifstream reader(filePath);
            if (!reader.is_open()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }
            nlohmann::ordered_json parsed = nlohmann::ordered_json::parse(reader);
            if (!parsed.is_object()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }
            return parsed;
        } catch (...) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
    }

    bool writeJson(const nlohmann::ordered_json& data, const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            std::ofstream writer(filePath);
            if (!writer.is_open()) {
                return false;
            }
            writer.exceptions(std::ofstream::failbit | std::ofstream::badbit);
            writer << data.dump();
            writer.flush();
            return true;
        } catch (...) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    bool processJson(const std::string& filePath, const std::string& removeKey) {
        auto data = readJson(filePath);
        if (!data) {
            return false;
        }
        if (!data->contains(removeKey)) {
            return false;
        }
        data->erase(removeKey);
        return writeJson(*data, filePath);
    }
};
```

```
void gsonify(nlohmann::ordered_json& j) {
    if (j.is_object()) {
        for (auto it = j.begin(); it != j.end(); ++it) {
            gsonify(it.value());
        }
    } else if (j.is_array()) {
        for (auto& item : j) {
            gsonify(item);
        }
    } else if (j.is_number()) {
        j = j.get<double>();
    }
}
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```cpp
#include <nlohmann/json.hpp>
#include <filesystem>
#include <fstream>
#include <optional>
#include <string>

class JSONProcessor {
public:
    std::optional<nlohmann::ordered_json> readJson(const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            if (!std::filesystem::exists(filePath)) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            std::ifstream reader(filePath);
            if (!reader.is_open()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            nlohmann::ordered_json parsed = nlohmann::ordered_json::parse(reader);
            if (!parsed.is_object()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            return parsed;
        } catch (...) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
    }

    bool writeJson(const nlohmann::ordered_json& data, const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            std::ofstream writer(filePath);
            if (!writer.is_open()) {
                return false;
            }

            writer.exceptions(std::ofstream::failbit | std::ofstream::badbit);
            writer << data.dump();
            writer.flush();
            writer.close();

            return true;
        } catch (...) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    bool processJson(const std::string& filePath, const std::string& removeKey) {
        auto data = readJson(filePath);
        if (!data) {
            return false;
        }

        if (!data->contains(removeKey)) {
            return false;
        }

        data->erase(removeKey);
        return writeJson(*data, filePath);
    }
};
```

=== TRANSLATED CODE (cpp) ===
#include <nlohmann/json.hpp>
#include <filesystem>
#include <fstream>
#include <optional>
#include <string>

class JSONProcessor {
public:
    std::optional<nlohmann::ordered_json> readJson(const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            if (!std::filesystem::exists(filePath)) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            std::ifstream reader(filePath);
            if (!reader.is_open()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            nlohmann::ordered_json parsed = nlohmann::ordered_json::parse(reader);
            if (!parsed.is_object()) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }

            return parsed;
        } catch (...) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
    }

    bool writeJson(const nlohmann::ordered_json& data, const std::string& filePath) {
        try {
            std::ofstream writer(filePath);
            if (!writer.is_open()) {
                return false;
            }

            writer.exceptions(std::ofstream::failbit | std::ofstream::badbit);
            writer << data.dump();
            writer.flush();
            writer.close();

            return true;
        } catch (...) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    bool processJson(const std::string& filePath, const std::string& removeKey) {
        auto data = readJson(filePath);
        if (!data) {
            return false;
        }

        if (!data->contains(removeKey)) {
            return false;
        }

        data->erase(removeKey);
        return writeJson(*data, filePath);
    }
};
